# Pre-pilot defect fixes — plan

**Status:** in build. **Branch:** `fix/prepilot-defects` (branched from `main` at `eaaa4ec`).
**Read this file first** at the start of every session, then `SESSION_LOG.md` next to it for
what actually happened, then `evidence/` for the classification this plan is derived from.

Fixing the 14 failure buckets found by re-analysing all 62 pre-pilot sessions (11–21 Aug 2026,
13 lawyers, 377 messages). 25 sessions FAIL, 16 carry a DEFECT, 21 PASS.

Full analysis, with the evidence for each bucket:
<https://claude.ai/code/artifact/43d8e63d-1c9d-4460-907c-2d35c1fa3786>

---

## How to use this file

1. Read **Invariants** — they are the things a fix must not break, and two of them are
   counter-intuitive enough that a cold session will get them wrong without reading.
2. Find the first unticked row in the **Ledger**. Rows are ordered by wave; do not start a
   wave before its predecessor is ticked, and do not start a row whose `Depends on` is unticked.
3. Each row is **self-contained**: files, the change, the acceptance test, and the sessions that
   evidence it. You should not need to re-derive anything from the transcripts to execute a row.
4. Follow the **Verification protocol**. A row is not tickable until its acceptance test passes.
5. Append to `SESSION_LOG.md` before you finish. The *Surprises* line is the one that matters.
6. If reality diverges from the plan, follow the **Re-planning protocol** — amend this file in
   the same commit as the work, do not leave the plan stale.
7. **When the user asks** (normally at the end of a session), update the **summary table**
   — the one-line-per-fix tracker shared with Thomas, at <https://claude.ai/artifact/JtrwLwRZnRihfe8kHj3EaJ>. Edit the `ROWS`
   array in `docs/prepilot-fixes/summary-table.html`, then republish that file with the
   Artifact tool, passing the URL above as `url` so the link stays the same. **Every row carries a `sev` (P1/P2/P3, defined on the page: P1 = the lawyer gets something wrong or misleading and cannot tell, or hits a dead end; P2 = correct but incomplete or harder to check; P3 = presentation, unseen wording or tooling).** It grades the defect, not the fix, so it does not change when a row is fixed. **Every row carries `added: "YYYY-MM-DD"`, shown in the Date added column (tracker v41, 2026-10-06, at the user's request): for our rows the first commit in which this file's ledger has the row (`git log --reverse -S "| **<ref>** |"`), for Thomas's the row's first appearance on the tracker, for both the earlier; a new row gets the day it is added.** **Every Fixed row also carries `fixed: "YYYY-MM-DD"`, shown in the Date fixed column (~~Fixed date~~, renamed in v41): the date of the first commit in which this file shows the row ticked `[x]` (the date it was accepted, not deployed). Add it when a row is ticked; the page does not compute it.** (Added 2026-09-24 at the user's request, tracker v16.) The table sorts itself ~~by severity, then status, then fixed date (oldest first)~~ with unfinished rows first, then by severity, then status, then fixed date (oldest first), so unfinished P1 rows lead (changed 2026-10-06 at the user's request, tracker v40); add rows anywhere in `ROWS`; plan order is the tie-break. The header shows the date and time of the update from the `AS_AT` constant beside `UPDATED`: set both on every update (added 2026-10-06 at the user's request, tracker v40). (~~status then severity~~, changed 2026-09-24 at the user's request, tracker v17.) The progress panel (overall bar, **by severity** since v18, by source) is computed from `ROWS` and needs no editing. (Added 2026-09-23 at the user's request.) **Statuses follow the ledger:** `[x]` is Fixed, `[~]` is In progress, and `[ ]` with confirmed evidence is Verified. **The exception is Partial** (tracker v23, 2026-09-25, at the user's request): a fix attempted and deferred, to be revisited later. **Only the user moves a row to Partial.** **Blocked** (tracker v34, user decision, 2026-10-05, at the user's request): a row parked on an external event; its tracker fix text ends "Blocked: <reason>", and the ledger keeps it `[ ]` with a BLOCKED note at the head of the row. P3.2, P3.3 and P3.17 are Blocked on the lawyer pack. The ledger has no marker for it, so a row can be `[~]` or `[x]` here and Partial on the tracker (P4.10 was `[~]` and Partial; Session 29 ticked it, and the user moved it to Fixed in tracker v24).

---

## Invariants

These hold for every row. Breaking one is a worse outcome than leaving a bucket unfixed.

1. **Honest failure is the most-praised behaviour in the corpus and must survive every fix.**
   Three lawyers praised it unprompted, two of them at 5/5 confidence; CambeulW gave 5/5 in
   session 6374 *because* AILA said it could not find something. Every fix to B3 and B5 must
   make the negatives **true** — it must not make the tool more willing to guess. A fix that
   raises the answer rate by lowering the evidence bar is a regression even if the ledger row
   goes green.

2. **Code enforcement beats prompt obedience.** This is already the house style (Phase-2 nudges,
   the parliamentary search budget, the `local_prompt_cache` drafting override). Where a row
   offers a choice between "tell the model to" and "make it so", take the second. B3(b), B7 and
   B12's disclosure footer are all specified this way deliberately.

3. **Do not change retrieval and measure in the same step.** Wave 1 fixes the input to every
   other measurement — while `_matches_jurisdiction` returns `False` for every real extent
   value, no retrieval-quality number means anything. Baseline first (Wave 0), then Wave 1,
   then re-baseline before Wave 3.

4. **A single replay pass is not evidence.** The model is stochastic. Acceptance for any row
   whose test involves an LLM is **n=3, all three clean** for a session classified FAIL, and
   **n=1** for one classified DEFECT (see *Replay configuration* — DEFECT symptoms are mostly
   deterministic, and an ambiguous one gets promoted to n=3). Rows marked *deterministic* have no
   model in the loop at all and need n=1 regardless.

5. **Additive and fail-soft, as everywhere else in this codebase.** No row here justifies a
   breaking change to a stored shape. New columns default; new fields are optional; a failure
   in a diagnostic must never fail the research run.

6. **The lawyers' own words are the ground truth, not the feedback scores.** Two sessions
   (6376, 6361) carry `Q7a = yes` with free text saying the opposite. Where a row cites a
   session, read the transcript, not the score.

## Verification protocol

Every row states its own acceptance test. Three kinds, and the distinction matters:

| Kind | What it means | n | Where it lives |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Deterministic** | No LLM. A unit test, or a static check over stored output. | 1 | `server_py/tests/` |
| **Replay** | Re-run the original session's question(s) through `/api/system/chat` and assert on the `audit` trace + answer. | 3 | `evidence/replay/` (gitignored output) |
| **External** | Answer comes from a third party, not from code. | — | `SESSION_LOG.md` |

### Iterate on a seam before you pay for a replay (Session 17, 2026-09-21)

**`python -m tools.seam_replay` is the cheap step before an acceptance sweep,
not a replacement for one.** Every fix since P2.2 has been a prompt or
composition change, and P3.1 established that retrieval upstream of them was
already correct — yet the iterate loop was a whole session replay. The tool
rebuilds ONE seam's input from a stored run file, using the product's own
builders, and makes the single model call:

```
python -m tools.seam_replay synthesis --run <run.json> --turn 1 [--without-fix]
python -m tools.seam_replay worker    --run <run.json> --turn 1 [--delegation K]
python -m tools.seam_replay <seam> --run … --dry-run     # payload only, no call
```

Measured on the fixtures it was built against: the **Worker composition** seam
costs **$0.03** a draw against $0.55 for a 6348 replay, and the **Deep Research
synthesis** seam **$0.11** against $0.61-0.79 for the turn. It needs no server.
`--without-fix` rebuilds the seam at an older revision (prompt constant read
from git, pinpoint block stripped), which is what makes an A/B possible.

**What it cannot do:** everything upstream is frozen — the retrieval is
whatever the recorded run retrieved — so it cannot test a retrieval change and
it cannot produce an acceptance. Use it to find the shape of a fix, then spend
on the n=3 replay once. **On a HALTED delegation the Worker seam closes with the
product's own write-up instruction (P3.8, Session 18), so a draw there IS the
write-up round the product makes at the step cap** — that is how P3.8's design
was settled for $0.21 before any code, and how its round is evidenced on
fixtures where a live replay may not halt at all. **`--from-raw` (P3.11,
Session 19) rebuilds each summarised result's appended blocks from its
recorded `raw_result` through `agent_shared.summarised_result_blocks`, the
product's own builder** — the seam replays `final_result`, so a block the
product builds from the raw result (P1.6's URLs, P3.11's outline) reaches it
only this way; the recorded payload is the before-column and `--from-raw`
the after. The pinned configuration runs at temperature 0, so one payload
gives one sample: count draws per payload, not draws. And the seam is an
approximation the other way too: P3.11's one live miss DELIVERED when its
own recorded payload was replayed here — a seam pass is not a live pass.

**The Manager seam (`seam_replay manager`, P3.13, Session 22) must offer the
Manager its tools, and does by default.** Tool-free, it DELIVERED in 3 of 3
draws a payload the live Manager had flattened (`wave3_p313` rep 1 turn 1).
Offered the tools the live call carries, it reproduced the miss in 3 of 3.
The earlier tool-free A/B had reported the fix as 3 of 3 on 6348; with the
tools it was 1 of 3, and the replay agreed with the harsher number. Neither
form is exact. Over the six recorded 6348 turn-1 payloads, each matched the
live outcome in 5. Temperature 0 is also not byte-deterministic on this model:
two draws of one payload came back at 1,466 and 1,488 chars. Take the harsher
reading before paying for a replay.

**Two more lessons from P3.13 (Session 22), for any prompt or answer-seam change.**
(1) **A prompt edit reaches every call that prompt drives, not only the one it
targets.** P3.13's CITATION PRESERVATION clause was meant for the Manager's
answer. It also changed the Manager's FIRST delegation brief, written before
any tool result, and one run went to the wrong Act. A first-round probe (the
system prompt and the question, with the tools offered, capturing the first
`delegate_research` call) separates that effect for a few cents. Probe each
call an edited prompt drives before paying for a replay.
(2) **`replay run --max-spend` is checked between reps, not within one.** A
P4.5 episode costs about $2.40 and 20 minutes in a single turn, so a $4 cap
recorded $5.65. Budget a 6348 n=3 at about $1–1.20 when healthy, and up to
$6 with P4.5 episodes. **It is also per command** (Session 40): each `replay run` counts only its own
runs, so a sweep of several commands needs its running total kept by hand against the
figure the user agreed (`sweep_total.py` in `evidence/seam/batch8/scratchpad_s40/`).
(3) **(Session 42) The default Worker seam draw is uncapped and retried.** `seam_replay worker` without
`--as-sent` goes through the product's `chat_loop` with no `max_tokens` and up to three attempts on an empty,
so a runaway draw costs up to about $2.30; price a seam draw at that, not at `--as-sent --max-tokens 32000`'s
$0.37. And a rep can run well past the stored maximum from a capped runaway alone (`wave4_b10_sweep` 6335 rep 1:
$0.99 against $0.61): when one more rep could take the running total past the agreed figure, stop the `replay
run` process between reps (the server cancels the in-flight request on disconnect; that rep's spend so far is
not recorded) and ask the user.

**Where the money goes, measured over `wave2`** (41 sessions, $29.55), for
scoping any future sweep: Deep Research turns are **20 of 155 turns and 48% of
the spend** ($0.71 each); 14 turns carry 45%; 8 sessions carry 53%; the
cheapest half of all turns costs $4.16 together. Other levers, none yet built:
replay only up to the graded turn, exclude Deep Research sessions from a row
that cannot touch them, reuse run files when `git diff <rev> HEAD --
server_py/src` is empty (done by hand twice in Session 16), and a cheap model
for plumbing smokes only — **never** for a graded run, where the pinned model
is the one the pre-pilot ran on.

### Replay configuration — decided 2026-09-14, do not vary without saying so in the log

| | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| **Model** | **Pin to `google/gemini-3.1-pro-preview` via OpenRouter** for every baseline and acceptance replay. | All **176** assistant messages in the pre-pilot ran on this model — uniform, no confound. The dev box is currently set to `moonshotai/kimi-k3` with `google/gemini-3-flash-preview` for summarisation; replaying on that measures a different system. `CLAUDE.md` already records model choice as the dominant variable (~10× swing on the same infrastructure). **First session must confirm the model is still served** — it is a preview model and may have been withdrawn. If it has, stop and re-decide; do not silently substitute. **Confirmed served 2026-09-14** (in catalogue, live tool-calling probe returns its own id; `replay.py check` re-runs this). |
| **Model pinning is a DB write, not a request field** | Set `app_settings → provider.openrouter → model`. `replay.py pin` does it and stashes the prior value for `restore`. | `system.py:129` resolves `provider_config.get("model") or body.model`, so the DB row **always** wins and `body.model` is a fallback that never fires. `audit["model"]` reports what actually executed and every run file asserts it against the pin. Sending the model in the request body and assuming it took effect would have silently measured `moonshotai/kimi-k3`. |
| **Summarisation model** | **Left at the dev box's `google/gemini-3-flash-preview` and recorded, not pinned** (user decision, 2026-09-14). | The pre-pilot's value is **not recoverable** — no per-message column records it and that database is not on this machine. Inventing one would be a silent confound in either direction; recording it keeps the gap visible. It is in `runtime_state` on every run file. |
| **Local prompt cache** | **Forced OFF for every replay sweep** (`replay.py pin`); restored afterwards. | It is cross-user and keyed on the raw tool output + canonicalised query, so run 1 populates it and runs 2..n read it back and skip summarisation. The repetitions would stop being independent draws, which is the entire point of n>1 under Invariant 4. `runtime_state` on each run file records the flag state it actually ran under. |
| **Repetitions** | ~~n=3 for the 25 FAIL sessions, n=1 for the 16 DEFECT sessions.~~ **Revised 2026-09-14 (user decision): n=1 across all 41 first, then reps 2–3 only on FAIL sessions whose first pass was ambiguous or disagreed with the frozen classification.** | The rep policy was costed at ~$50; replay now costs ~2.3× the pre-pilot's recorded spend, making a blanket n=3/n=1 pass ~$140 (see *Cost*). Targeted repetition spends the draws where the uncertainty actually is — a session that reproduces verbatim on the first pass does not need three — and keeps Invariant 4's intent, which is that no **FAIL verdict** rests on a single sample. Sessions promoted to n=3 must be named in `BASELINE.md` with the reason. |
| **Cost** | ~**$27** per single full pass *at pre-pilot rates*; **measured ≈2.3× that today**, so ~**$60** for one pass over all 41 and ~**$70–90** for the targeted-n=3 baseline. A blanket n=3/n=1 pass would be ~**$140**, not the ~$50 originally stated. | ~~$50 for the n=3/n=1 baseline~~ — that figure assumed replay cost equals the pre-pilot's recorded per-message spend. It does not: the recorded cost covers only the saved assistant message, while a replay also pays for the Deep Research **planner** call (44 of them in a blanket pass, saved as no message and therefore absent from the CSV) and for whatever extra tool work the current HEAD does. Measured ratio over the first five sessions: 1.04×–8.90×, aggregate 2.34×. Record actual spend per pass in `SESSION_LOG.md` so the estimate stays honest. |
| **Credentials** | OpenRouter key is in the **local DB**, not `.env` — `app_settings` row `provider.openrouter`. `active_provider` is `openrouter`. | Settings are DB-first by design; `.env` holds only startup fallbacks. Change the model via Admin Portal → Developer, or write the `provider.openrouter` row directly. **Restore the dev box's previous model afterwards** (`replay.py restore`) so unrelated work is not silently running on the pinned model. |
| **Auth** | `/api/system/chat` requires `get_current_user` (`routers/system.py:76`). Log in at `/api/auth/login` and send `Authorization: Bearer <token>`. | The July 2026 auth hardening locked every endpoint and there is no global middleware. ~~carry the cookie~~ — the cookie works, but `POST /api/auth/login` also returns the token in the response body and `get_current_user`'s header branch exists specifically for machine callers (`auth.py:118-122` says so). The header is the documented machine path and avoids the `secure`-flag/scheme coupling entirely. |
| **Deep Research needs two calls** | A DR turn must `POST /api/research/plan` first, then send the returned plan as `deep_research_plan`. | `/api/system/chat` returns **HTTP 400** for `chat_mode="deep_research"` with no plan, by design, so a harness cannot bank a standard research run as a DR result. **The pre-pilot's approved plans are not recoverable** (`messages.research_plan`, on a database not present here), so a replayed DR turn runs a freshly drafted plan — adding planner stochasticity the original did not have. The drafted plan is saved in the run file. |
| **Chat mode per turn** | ~~Deep Research from the answer's marker; otherwise the feedback snapshot's `Filter: Chat mode`, else `research`.~~ **From 2026-09-22 (P0.5): Deep Research from the marker; otherwise from the recorded answer's shape — never a default.** | The default was a guess, and it was wrong for 12 of 41 sessions (48 of 155 turns): the export's mode field is blank for the sessions run before it existed (11–13 August), the lawyers ran them in Conversational mode with the Research flag off, and their recorded answers carry the research Worker's headings 0 times in 38. Found 2026-09-22 when the user asked why any turn was Research with the flag off. See P0.5. **Done, Session 20: sources are now `dr_marker` 27, `snapshot` 108, `conversational_marker` 52 and `neighbour` 9 over the 62 sessions, and no turn falls through to a default.** A turn that got no reply takes the nearest answered non-Deep-Research turn of its own session; `DEFAULT_CHAT_MODE` is now `conversational` and is unreachable here. `python -m tools.replay_report modes --dir <D>` prints the per-turn mode and its evidence, the ran-as × answer-shape cross-tab and the export's own answers, and **exits 1** if a guessed mode or a mode mismatch reached the directory — run it on any new sweep before quoting a number from it. **The one field still carrying a default is `research_mode`: see P0.6.** |

**The replay harness is `/api/system/chat` with `emit_tool_details=True`**, which emits the
structured `audit` event (`delegations[] → tools[] → api_calls[]`, with `raw_result` alongside
`final_result`). Spec: `docs/api/AUDIT_TRACE.md`. It exists precisely so a harness can see what
was *retrieved*, not just what was answered — which is what most of these rows assert on.
Do **not** build a new harness against `/api/chat`; it does not emit tool events.

`request_timings.source` is already tagged `"eval"` for this endpoint, so replay traffic stays
separable from live traffic in the Efficiency and Cache tabs. Note the dashboards do not yet
filter on it, so a large replay sweep moves the headline numbers — expected, not a bug.

## Re-planning protocol

This plan is expected to change. Two rules:

- **Amend the plan in the same commit as the work that invalidated it.** A session that
  discovers a row is wrong edits the row, adds a `~~strikethrough~~` note saying what was
  believed and what is now known, and says so in `SESSION_LOG.md`. A stale plan is the specific
  failure mode this structure exists to prevent.
- **New findings become new rows, not scope creep inside an existing row.** If executing P1.1
  uncovers a second defect, add P1.5 rather than widening P1.1 — otherwise the ledger stops
  reflecting what was actually verified.

Wave 5 rows are **external** and have unknown lead times. Open them early and in parallel;
they are not blockers for anything in Waves 0–4, **except P3.20 (Wave 3), which builds what P5.2 decides (booked 2026-10-02)**.

### Wave 1 — The proven code defects (deterministic, no model behaviour change)

| | ID | Row |
|---|---|---|
| `[x]` | **P1.1** | **B2 — Fix the jurisdiction filter.** `_matches_jurisdiction` (`server_py/src/agent/tools/lex.py:44`) splits `extent` on `+` and tests single-letter tokens `E`/`W`/`S`/`NI`. The LEX API returns full territory names. Verified live: the six values the API emits are `['']` (68), `['Scotland']` (42), `['England','Wales']` (4), `['England','Wales','Scotland']` (3), `['United Kingdom']` (2), `['Northern Ireland']` (1) — all six return False for every jurisdiction value. ~~so the filter discards 100% of results~~ **Corrected by the P0.3 baseline, and the correction matters: the filter does not fail closed, it fails *selectively*.** `_matches_jurisdiction` opens `if not extent: return True`, so rows with a **missing** extent pass. Of the **1,009 rows that survived** a jurisdiction filter across the whole baseline, **every one had `extent: []`** and **not one carrying a real extent value survived** — a Scotland filter drops `['Scotland']` and keeps the unknowns (6354 returned the *Building Materials and Housing Act 1945* under `jurisdiction=scotland`). That is why 13 sessions ran with it and nobody reported a broken filter: it returns a plausible non-empty result set that is wrong rather than an obviously empty one. **Measured baseline: 351 of 974 searches (36%) returned nothing to the model, 478 (49%) demonstrably lost rows, ≥2,147 rows lost; 0 of 557 searches lost anything with the filter off.** So the row has **two** jobs, not one: map the real vocabulary, **and decide what `extent: []` means** — leaving unknown-and-include is what makes the failure invisible. `['']` should join it (most SSIs carry it). `uk_wide` currently requires `tokens ⊇ {E,W,S,NI}` and is equally unreachable; decide what it means against `['United Kingdom']`. **Decide explicitly** that UK-wide and assimilated (`eur`) instruments are in scope for a Scotland filter — that is what CambeulW asked for in 6406 and it is the difference between a usable filter and a trap. **Acceptance** *(deterministic)*: unit test asserting True for each live extent value against its own territory, pinned to a fixture of the live vocabulary so an API change breaks the test not the product; plus a `jurisdiction=scotland` search for "Courts Reform (Scotland) Act 2014" returning `asp/2014/18`; plus `replay_report` showing 0 searches emptied by the filter on a re-run of 6354/6381/6408. **Evidence:** 6408 6384 6383 6382 6381 6396 6359 6407 6354 6357. **Depends on:** P0.3 · **DONE.** Policy decided with the user 2026-09-14: the control means **"applies in"**, not "made for" — Scottish-extent + UK-wide + unknown-extent, minus other-jurisdiction id prefixes. Scored over the baseline's 17,560 real rows it drops **0%** of unambiguously Scottish rows and admits **0** clearly non-Scottish ones, against the old code's **97.5% dropped**. Note `['']` had to be treated as unknown-and-include: matching territory names alone would have dropped 40% of Scottish material (2,009 Scottish SIs carry `['']`) — a new trap. The id-prefix tie-break (`nisr/`, `wsi/`) is what makes admitting unknowns safe. **Acceptance passed:** `tests/test_jurisdiction_filter.py` (42 tests, live vocabulary pinned as a fixture); live `jurisdiction=scotland` search for "Courts Reform (Scotland) Act 2014" keeps 20/20 incl. `asp/2014/18`, where the old code kept **0/20**.
| `[x]` | **P1.2** | **B4 (filter half) — Stop the `current_only` filter pretending.** `_INACTIVE` (`executor.py:211–216`) tests for `{repealed, revoked, spent, expired, not in force}`; ~~the API's `status` vocabulary is `final` and `revised` only~~ — **corrected at P2.5: it is `final` 60.3%, `revised` 38.8% and `stub` 0.9%** over 15,160 model-visible rows (`lex_probe --inforce`), three values rather than two, and none of them an in-force signal — so nothing is ever excluded. The field means *which text version is held*, not in-force status, so this **cannot be fixed by extending the word list**. Two parts: (a) remove or relabel the filter so it does not promise currency it cannot deliver; (b) strip in-force assertions from answers — see P2.5 for the model-side half. **Do not** silently leave the toggle in the UI doing nothing. *Open product call when this row is reached:* remove the toggle outright or relabel it to what it actually does. Recommendation is **remove** — a relabelled filter that still excludes nothing is a second trap, and 42 of 62 sessions ran with this on believing it worked. **Acceptance** *(deterministic)*: unit test pinning the live status vocabulary; a search with the filter on returns the same set as with it off, *or* the filter is gone. **Evidence:** 6378 6341 6411. **Depends on:** P0.3 · **DONE — removed, per the user's decision 2026-09-14.** **The row understated this: the filter was not merely a no-op, it was actively asserting currency in two places.** The UI pill read *"In force as at <today>"*, and `build_filter_constraint_block` injected *"Status: In-force legislation only. Do not cite or rely on repealed or not-yet-in-force legislation."* into the system prompt. 42 of 62 sessions ran with that on, so **this is the proximate cause of bucket B4** and it partly pre-empts P2.5 — the model was reporting currency because the system told it the results were current. Removed: the toggle, the pill, the prompt claim, the no-op post-filter **and the request field** (option C, user 2026-09-15). Safe for clients because `AgentRequestBase` does not set `extra="forbid"` — pydantic ignores an unknown key rather than rejecting it, so a client still sending `current_only` gets the same 200 and the value goes nowhere. `audit["filters"]["current_only"]` is deliberately **kept, always null**, so the trace shape is unchanged for the external eval harness and `AUDIT_SCHEMA_VERSION` stays at 1; the natural moment to go to v2 is P2.1, which changes the trace shape anyway. `docs/api/AUDIT_TRACE.md` updated. **Acceptance passed:** `tests/test_current_only_removed.py`; frontend rebuilt.
| `[x]` | **P1.3** | **B5 (data half) — Stop discarding what the search actually found.** Two changes in `executor.py`: (a) `_TYPE_CODES` (`lex.py:80`) has no `eur` entry, so `legislation_type` drops every assimilated EU regulation — confirmed present and retrievable (`eur/2009/1069` returns 10 sections); (b) `executor.py:227` sets `slimmed["total"] = len(results)` **after** post-filtering, destroying the API's real match count. **Baseline: 1,010 of 1,531 searches misreport `total`, including 438 of 557 with *no* jurisdiction or type filter — so this is not a side-effect of B2 and does not go away if P1.1 lands first.** **Measurement warning:** `executor.py` also caps every search to `results[:5]` *after* filtering. That cap is deliberate slimming, not a defect; any "rows lost" figure that ignores it attributes ordinary truncation to the filters and overstates B2 several-fold. `replay_report.RESULT_CAP` encodes it — a search returning exactly 5 is cap-bound and is evidence of nothing. Return `returned` / `total_matched` / `removed_by_filters` as separate fields so the model can tell "5 matched" from "172 matched and your filters removed all but 5". This is the D16 defect-2 fix, now with field evidence. **Acceptance** *(deterministic)*: unit test asserting the three counts are distinct and correct for a filtered search; `legislation_type=secondary` no longer drops an `eur/` result, or `eur` is deliberately excluded with the reason in a comment. **Evidence:** 6406 6408 + the whole of B5. **Depends on:** P0.3 · **DONE.** `_TYPE_CODES` gains `eur`/`eudn`/`eudr` (assimilated EU law sits with secondary — directly-applicable law made outside Parliament), plus `nisro`/`nisi`/`sdsi` which were missing for the same reason. Counts now reported as `returned` / `total_matched` / `removed_by_filters`, with `filters_applied` naming the filters when any removed rows, and `total` restored to the API's real match count. **Acceptance passed:** `tests/test_search_result_shape.py`.

Corrected 2026-09-15 during P1.5: **20 of 376 (5.3%)**, worst offenders 6406 (16/61), 6375 (3/15), 6389 (0/18), 6341 (0/53). B14 is real but roughly a quarter of the size first published, and 16 of the 20 sit in one session. **P1.4's acceptance is unaffected** — it passed on `tests/test_search_result_shape.py`, which tests the product. Note the warning: `test_bad_link_*` in `tests/test_replay_tooling.py` was green the whole time, because it used synthetic labels that never contained a real SI title. Build the URL from the provision identifier rather than letting the model compose it. HeatherE asked for exactly this in 6334. Lowest-effort row on this page and it directly serves the click-through verification the lawyers actually use. **Acceptance** *(deterministic)*: static check over any replay output — every link whose label names a section/regulation/article/schedule contains the matching path segment. **Evidence:** 6334 6409 6375 6341 6348. **Depends on:** P0.3 · **DONE, and the cause was our own prompt.** The API already returns a provision-level URL per result (`uri`), but `search_legislation_sections` passed its response through raw and `WORKER_SYSTEM_PROMPT` told the model *"you MUST manually append `/section/{number}` to the Base URI"* — it was obeying an instruction to guess. New `_slim_section_results` hands over the API's own URL under a `url` key (also normalising `legislation_id`, which the two LEX endpoints spell differently), and both citation protocols now say to use it verbatim. Covers schedules, regulations and articles, not just sections. **Acceptance passed:** `tests/test_search_result_shape.py`. **Re-measured at P1.5, and the headline metric misreports it.** `bad_links` went **20 to 32**, which reads as a regression and is not: that metric asks whether a link matches the *granularity* its label names, and cannot ask whether the URL was ever **returned by a tool**. On the question that matters, ~~provision URLs that no tool ever returned went **327 of 327 (100%) to 26 of 136 (19%)**.~~ **Corrected at P1.6 — that reading was the instrument again.** It asked whether the URL appeared in `final_result`, which is the **summarised** text whenever summarisation fired, and 70% of section searches are summarised — the summary keeps the section numbers and drops every URL. Split three ways (shown to the model / reconstructed after summarisation ate it / never returned at all), rep-1 like-for-like: shown **5 → 110**, reconstructed **293 → 26**, and provision URLs no tool returned **anywhere** **29 → 0**. P1.4 did more than was published, not less — it slimmed the payload enough that 81% of cited provision URLs are now copied verbatim from a result the model was actually shown, against 1.5% before. The old prompt *told* the model to append `/section/{number}`, so nearly every Wave 0 provision URL was composed rather than copied, and most carried the right number so the checker scored them **good**. A manufactured URL resolves to a real page and reads to a lawyer as a verified citation, which is worse than a link that misses its provision. The 32 flagged links are the residue of honesty: with no retrieved provision URL and forbidden from inventing one, the model links the Act's contents page while naming a section — in 6348, **zero** provision URLs were returned by any tool yet it cites FOISA ss.36 and 55, so the bad link is now a *symptom of citing an unretrieved provision* rather than a formatting fault. The dev box was deliberately **restored** after the Wave 0 sweep, so it is currently on `moonshotai/kimi-k3`; running without pinning measures a different system and the runner will refuse unless you pass `--allow-unpinned`. **(2)** Start uvicorn **fresh** — `python -m uvicorn src.main:app --host 127.0.0.1 --port 8000 --no-access-log`. *Python loads modules at import*, so a server left running from a previous session serves the OLD code and would record pre-Wave-1 behaviour as the Wave 1 result. Confirm with `python -m tools.replay check`. **(3)** `python -m tools.replay run --all --reps 1 --out-dir ../docs/prepilot-fixes/evidence/replay/wave1` — **a NEW directory**. `run` skips any run file that already exists, so pointing it at `…/replay/baseline` silently skips all 65 and does nothing. ~$38, ~6 h serial. **(4)** `python -m tools.replay_report --dir ../docs/prepilot-fixes/evidence/replay/wave1 summary`, then `python -m tools.replay restore`. **Read it session-by-session, not as two headline numbers:** a verdict can flip for the wrong reason. 6381 currently reads *does not reproduce* because it honestly says it cannot find the Victims and Witnesses (Scotland) Act 2014 — and the reason it cannot is B2 emptying its searches. With P1.1 in it may now answer, which looks like a Wave 1 win but is the removal of a cause already recorded. **Numbers to watch:** B2's 351 emptied searches → expect 0; B14's 88/376 bad provision links → expect a collapse; **B4's 27 in-force claims is the interesting one** — P1.2 removed the prompt line that caused them, so a sharp fall means P2.5's scope shrinks before it is written. **Acceptance:** `BASELINE.md` gains a second column. **Depends on:** P1.1–P1.4 · **DONE 2026-09-15.** 41 sessions / 155 turns on the Wave 1 HEAD, **$27.22 / 3.7 h** against $37.62 / 6.0 h, zero model mismatches, zero errored turns, no product code changed during the sweep. **B2 and B5 closed**: searches emptied by filters **351/1,531 to 3/790** (the 3 are `legislation_type=primary` correctly excluding SSIs, checked row by row); `total` misreported **1,010 to 0**. **B2 was also a cost and latency defect** — searches **1,531 to 790**, delegations 278 to 197, and the sweep ran 2.3 h faster for the same turns, because an emptied search made the model reformulate and retry (6396 172 searches to 1, 6381 80 to 6). **B4 rose 27 to 30, so P2.5's scope does NOT shrink** — the prompt instructs the claim at three sites P1.2 never touched. **Silent halts rose 2 to 5** while halt text fell 9 to 6, with no code change: stochastic, and the exact trade P2.1's rewritten acceptance guards against. **Cap pressure rose** (p90 15 to 20; at-or-over-cap 6.3% to 11.2%) — see P2.1. Three detectors were corrected mid-sweep (B14 six-fold over-count, B1 blind to a third of halt disclosures, B8 conflating two conditions); both columns use the corrected instrument. **n=1 throughout, so no session is recorded as fixed** — Invariant 4. |

| `[x]` | **P1.6** | **B14 residual — enforce the provision URL in code, not in the prompt.** *(New row from P1.5's re-baseline, per the re-planning protocol: P1.4 is done and accepted, this is a separate, newly-measurable defect.)* ~~P1.4 cut manufactured provision URLs from **100% to 19%**… The residual 19% is the model disobeying that instruction where it holds only an Act-level URL — 6365 appends `/section/21` and `/section/22` to `asp/2000/1`, for provisions no tool retrieved.~~ **That premise was the instrument, not the product — the ninth trap, and it was reading a third of the answer.** `_urls_returned_by_tools` asked only whether the URL appeared in `final_result`, which is the **summarised** text whenever summarisation fired — and **70% of section searches are summarised**, the summary keeping the section numbers and dropping every URL. 6365's `/section/21` and `/section/22` **were** returned by `search_legislation_sections`; they survive in `raw_result` and were eaten before the model saw them. Corrected, there are **three** outcomes, not two: *shown* (copied verbatim), *reconstructed* (the tool returned it, the summariser dropped it, the model rebuilt it — substantiated but built by guessing), *manufactured* (no tool returned it anywhere — the provision was never retrieved). Rep-1 like-for-like, Wave 0 → Wave 1: shown 5 → 110, reconstructed 293 → 26, **manufactured 29 → 0**. So the published "327/327 (100%) → 26/136 (19%)" conflated the last two and **understated** what P1.4 achieved. **What P1.6 therefore fixes is the mechanism, not a live wrong answer**: 19% of cited provision links are still *rebuilt* rather than copied, they happen to be right today, and nothing made them so. **Invariant 2 applies exactly as written: stop instructing, start enforcing.** **Built, both halves** (`src/utils/citation_links.py`): **(a) the cause** — `provision_url_block` appends the retrieval's own provision URLs *after* summarisation, for the same reason the Phase-2 nudge is appended there (the summariser cannot eat what it never saw), so the model copies instead of rebuilding; **(b) the guarantee** — `enforce_provision_links` runs at the worker-report seam, the Manager's final-answer seam and the Deep Research synthesis seam, against a **request-scoped** set of every legislation.gov.uk URL any tool returned (harvested from the RAW result, and shared across delegations and DR steps because a provision retrieved in step 2 is legitimately cited in step 5). A provision URL not in that set is **demoted to the Act when the Act was retrieved** and unlinked otherwise, with one footnote. ~~Prefer (b)~~ — both, and (b) is not "a filter over model output" in the drift sense: it is keyed on retrieval provenance, not on wording. **The row's warning held and shaped the design:** the fix must not simply strip links (when only the Act is held, linking the Act is correct — the error is the label naming a provision), and the marker must be **true**: we know no tool returned a URL for the citation; we do **not** know the provision went unread, because a whole-Act `get_legislation_text` carries one URL and no per-section ones. So the footnote asserts only what is known about the link (Invariant 1). **Acceptance** *(deterministic)*: ~~`provision_links_manufactured == 0` over a replay directory, plus a unit test~~ — the first half was **already true at Wave 1** once the detector was corrected, so on its own it would have passed without any code being written. **PASSED on the restated test:** `tests/test_citation_links.py` (28 tests, incl. end-to-end through `run_worker_agent`: identical model output, identical report, retrieved → untouched vs not-retrieved → demoted) plus `tests/test_replay_tooling.py`'s seven new provenance tests, which pin the corrected detector *and* pin that P1.6's own block counts as **shown** — without that last one the fix would have been invisible to its own metric. `provision_links_manufactured == 0` holds over `evidence/replay/wave1` and is now enforced by code rather than observed. **Watch `provision_links_reconstructed` (26 at Wave 1) on the next sweep — that is the number this row moves**, and it needs a Wave 2 sweep, not this row. **Evidence:** 6365 6348 6407. **Depends on:** P1.5 |

### Wave 2 — Structural honesty (changes what the model may say, not what it can find)

| | ID | Row |

| `[x]` | **P2.1** | **B1 — The research halt must stop being a legal finding.** ~~"The **Deep Research** halt"~~ — **the halt is not confined to Deep Research.** `chat_loop` returns `"[Research halted: exceeded {max_turns} tool-call steps]"` as the worker's **assistant content** (`agent/openrouter_client.py:158`, `agent/ollama_client.py:85`, `max_turns=20`), so it escapes by **both** routes: `run_deep_research` (`agent_core.py:698`) treats it as a step's findings and hands it to synthesis, **and** a plain Manager `delegate_research` gets it as the worker's report. Confirmed in baseline replay of **6340**, a standard `research`-mode session (`kind: "delegation"`, no step), where a 30-tool-call worker halted and the lawyer was told the agent *"exceeded its operational limits (timed out)"*. **A fix at the `run_deep_research` site alone leaves the Manager path open.** **10 of 23 DR sessions (43%)**, plus an unmeasured share of standard sessions — count them from the baseline. Return the halt as structured metadata on the step/worker result (`{complete: false, reason: "step_budget"}`); synthesis and the Manager must not render it as a legal gap; the UI marks the step incomplete. **Invariant 1 applies with a sharp edge here:** 6340's halt answer *reads* as honest failure but says "timed out" (it hit a step cap, not a timeout) and then invents a cause — *"a broad enabling power has generated a very large volume of statutory instruments over several decades"* — for an Act that is a **404 in LEX**. The disclosure must be true, not merely non-legal. **Read `request_timings.max_turns_halted` (already collected, `utils/stopwatch.py:177`, already aggregated at `routers/stats.py:673`) before changing the cap** — the cap question is separate from the presentation question and only one of them is urgent. **Cap pressure measured at P1.5, and Wave 1 moved it — do not decide this on the Wave 0 numbers.** Over the 30 sessions of the Wave 1 sweep, like-for-like against the same sessions in the rep-1 baseline: delegations **190 to 134** (-30%, the model stopped flailing against empty searches), but **p90 tool calls per delegation 15 to 20 — the 90th-percentile delegation now sits AT the cap** — and delegations at or over 20 went **6.3% to 11.2%** (12 to 15 in absolute terms, while the denominator fell by a third). Median 4 to 5, max 41 to 45. **Fixing retrieval nearly doubled the share of delegations hitting the ceiling**, because a search that returns results generates Phase-2 retrieval work where an emptied search just got retried. This is Invariant 3 one level up: the cap was parked behind a metric that was itself measured against a broken filter. **Still do not raise it reflexively.** Hitting the cap is not self-evidently wrong — a delegation needing 25 steps may be badly scoped rather than starved, and 20 to 30 would add cost and latency to the ~11% without necessarily improving an answer, while *masking* the very failure this row exists to make honest. What has changed is that the cap is now **binding** rather than a rarely-touched safety net, so it is a live design decision. Decide it here, with the presentation fix, on the completed sweep — not before. (Numbers: n=1, 30 of 41 sessions, taken mid-sweep with the halt-heavy sessions 6382, 6384, 6396, 6407, 6408 still outstanding; refresh before deciding.) ~~**Acceptance** *(replay, n=3)*: 6406, 6382, 6408 and 6340 produce no halt text and no invented cause in the report body.~~ **REWRITTEN 2026-09-15 during P1.5 — the old test was broken three ways and one of them made it actively harmful.** **(a) "No halt text" is the wrong assertion.** It is satisfied by saying nothing, and **2 turns in the rep-1 baseline (3 across all runs) already halt silently** — a worker halts, a normal-looking report comes back, the lawyer gets no signal it is incomplete (6396 rep1 halted **four** workers and never mentions it). Under Invariant 1 that is worse than the text leaking, so the trivially-passing implementation of the old test is the worse product. **(b) The detector was blind to a third of the wordings** — `HALT_PARAPHRASE` caught 9 of 14 real disclosures, missing "exceeded its *processing* limits", "timed out while searching", "exceeded the maximum *permitted steps*" — so the assertion could pass on a paraphrase it did not know. Widened and re-validated at P1.5. **(c) 6406 no longer halts at all** (0 of 4 steps in all three reps), so it cannot evidence the fix; **6382 and 6384 still halt and still leak**, and **6383 turn 1 is the strongest case** — the raw string as its entire answer, and note it **never appears in any delegation report**, so a check reading only `delegations[].report` is blind to it. **New acceptance** *(replay, n=3, on 6382, 6384, 6383 and 6340 — not 6406)*: for every turn whose worker halted, (1) `halts_undisclosed == 0` — the answer *does* tell the lawyer the research was incomplete; (2) no raw `[Research halted` string anywhere in the answer; (3) the stated reason is **true** — a step cap, not "timed out"; (4) no invented cause (6340's *"a broad enabling power has generated a very large volume of statutory instruments"* for an Act that 404s in LEX); (5) the halt is present as structured metadata on the step/worker result. Assert on the **answer**, not only the trace. 6406 is the acceptance case — all four steps halted and the report cost $2.36 to say nothing. **Evidence:** 6406 6408 6382 6384 6409 6374 6407 6389 6357 6341 6340 — and note **6409 turn 7 is the starkest case in the corpus, stronger than 6340**: 25 Phase-1 searches, 0 retrievals, the worker hit the cap, and the entire answer was *"The research agent could not locate [the SSI] in the legislation database"*. A step cap rendered as a **negative retrieval finding**, with no hint the research was cut off — an *untrue* honest failure, which is the one thing Invariant 1 cannot survive. **Depends on:** P1.5 · **DONE (Session 5) — code enforcement at three seams, and the cap decision went the other way.** **Built:** `chat_loop` (both providers) now returns `{"halted": {reason, limit, steps}}` alongside the marker, so the halt travels as structure; `run_worker_agent` replaces a halted report with a statement addressed to the agent that will read it (modelled on the proven `[Research Agent Error]` containment string) which forbids the two behaviours 6340 showed — calling it a timeout, and inventing a cause; `process_user_request` **and** `run_deep_research` strip every raw marker and **prepend** a code-emitted notice naming the plan steps where known. All three escape routes covered, including the Manager's own loop. **The disclosure is unconditional** — no detector decides whether the model already disclosed it well enough, because a false negative there is a lawyer relying on a partial answer, and on this work the detectors have been wrong more often than the product. Rides out as `research_incomplete` on the result (additive, carried by the existing `result` SSE event, no router change) and as `delegations[].halted` in the audit trace — **`AUDIT_SCHEMA_VERSION` bumped to 2**, spec updated, and the test now pins the constant rather than a literal. `src/utils/research_halt.py`. **The UI half is satisfied by the text, deliberately, and a badge would be worse:** `research_incomplete` has no DB column, so a badge would vanish on reload while the prepended notice — which lives in `messages.content` — persists. A marker that disappears on reload teaches the lawyer that its absence means complete. **THE CAP: NOT RAISED, and the row's own figures were the wrong counter.** `max_turns` caps **ReAct rounds**; a round issues several tool calls in parallel (`asyncio.gather`) and the Worker prompt instructs batching, so counting tool calls against 20 measures a different quantity — 6341 turn 6 made **45 tool calls in 13 rounds**. On the **completed** 41-session sweep, using `react_turns_max` (the counter the cap acts on): p90 **17 → 13**, at-cap **7.8% → 7.2%**. Pressure **fell**; it did not nearly double. And the turns that do hit it are **looping, not starved** — 26% redundant tool calls against a 15% base rate, with 6409 spending 22 and 25 Phase-1 searches for 1 and 0 retrievals. More rounds buys more of the same and would mask the failure this row exists to make honest. Full working: `BASELINE.md` → *The step cap*. That evidence opens **P2.7**. **Acceptance:** four of the five conditions are mechanical and are checked by `replay_report … halts` (new subcommand, per **turn** — a session's failure lives in one turn of four); the fifth (no invented cause) is a reading of the prose, printed by `--answers`. Wave 1 grades **11 of 11 halted turns FAILING** as the before-state: **6 of 11 say nothing at all**, and of the 5 that do mention it, 2 call it a timeout and 1 discloses it by printing the raw marker as the answer — so **2 of 11 were disclosed acceptably**, and none carried the metadata. **ACCEPTANCE PASSED** — replay n=3 on 6382, 6384, 6383, 6340 → `evidence/replay/wave2_p21/` (12 runs, **$8.63**, 0 errored turns, 0 model mismatches). **7 halted turns, 0 failing** on all four mechanical conditions, and reading the prose for the fifth, **none invented a cause** — 6340's *"a broad enabling power has generated a very large volume of statutory instruments"* is gone from all three of its replays. **State the coverage honestly: only 6340 and 6382 give full n=3 evidence** (both halted in 3 of 3 reps); 6383 halted in 1 of 3 and **6384 in 0 of 3**, so their condition is vacuous rather than passed, and "four sessions × three reps" would read as four times the evidence it is. Note also that **6383 turn 1 — the row's named strongest case — did not halt in any rep**; the halt moved to turn 4's DR plan. That is why the grading is per halted **turn** over the directory rather than per named session. The model's own prose came along too (not assumed, since the disclosure is code-emitted): all 7 turns state the true reason in the model's words, and 6383 rep 1 adds its own *"this is not a negative result or evidence that the material does not exist"*. **One residual, booked to P2.2 rather than widened into this row:** 6382 rep 2 still opens with a negative (*"no SSIs … were found that contain the '£' symbol"*) drawn from two steps that halted — now contradicted in plain sight by the notice, but not yet prevented. **A second residual, observed at Session 13 in P2.8's sweep. It is not a new row at n=1.** In `wave2_p28/6341 rep 1 turn 7`, a Deep Research synthesis whose four steps all halted writes *"because the research steps timed out due to internal limits"*, directly beneath the code notice saying the halt is **not** a timeout. `halts` grades it **FAIL**. It is the first such failure in **24 halted turns** across the post-P2.1 directories (`wave2_p21`, `wave2_p22_final`, `wave2_p23`, `wave3_p35`, `wave2_p25`, `wave2_p28`). **Recounted at Session 14:** the five P2.4 directories (`wave2_p24_pre`, `wave2_p24_smoke`, `wave2_p24`, `wave2_p24_ab`, `wave2_p24_final`) have **0 halted turns** between them, because none of 6373, 6375 or 6385 halts. So the residual is still 1 FAIL in 24 halted turns, and still not worth building for. The code notice is present and correct; the contradiction is in the model's prose. **Run `halts` on every new sweep and recount before building anything for it.** **Recounted at Session 15 over all eighteen post-P2.1 directories: 1 FAIL in 42 halted turns.** The "24" above counted six directories; `wave2_p22` and `wave2_p28_smoke` add 4 halted turns it never included, and P2.7's three directories add 14, all passing. Still the only failure, and still not worth building for. It was missed at first in Session 13 because `halts` was not run on the acceptance directory. **Recounted at Session 18 over every post-P2.1 directory: 1 FAIL in 43 halted turns** (`wave3_p38_pre` has 0 halted turns; `wave3_p38` has 1, passing). **P3.8 (Session 18) changed what a halted worker hands back, not the disclosure:** at the cap `chat_loop` now makes one tool-free write-up round, and a halted report is this row's header (now saying the findings are PARTIAL) above those findings; `halted.written_up` (audit v4) records whether the round produced anything; the lawyer's notice is byte-for-byte unchanged and still prepended by code.

Wave 1's 6409 turn 7 is the same failure without the disclosure. **Depends on:** P1.3, P2.1 · **DONE (Session 6) — and the row's own premise was 0.5% of the bucket.** **What the measurement said before any code was written:** the missing `else:` on `if id_pairs:` fires on **4 of 790** searches post-Wave-1, while **783 of 783** measurable searches are *windowed* (5 rows shown of a median **141** ranked candidates, p90 185, max 220) and **not one of the 44 measured bare negatives followed an empty search**. Every one came from a result set that had results, just not the wanted one — *"no commencement regulations have been made"* is seven of them verbatim, drawn from the top 5 of 141. A fix confined to the empty branch would have moved none of the forty-four and could still have gone green on a loose detector. **Built, at three seams, and the second was found by the acceptance failing:** (a) a scope block on **every** `search_legislation` result — query, filters, the window, and the coverage limb — plus the first nudge `search_legislation_sections` has ever had (6335 turn 7 reported a targeted search "returned no results" when it had returned results, then invented *"the database is having difficulty parsing the Schedule B1 structure"*); (b) `worker_scope_block`, appended in code to the **worker's report**, because the Worker sees tool results while the **Manager sees only the report and the DR synthesis only the step findings** — whoever writes the BLUF had never seen a scope block (6367 rep 1 carried the block in context **27 times** and still said a search *"confirms"* that no SSIs prescribe the detail); (c) `answer_scope_footer`, a one-line lawyer-facing statement emitted by code on every researched answer, plus an unconditional strip so the agent-facing blocks never render. `src/utils/search_scope.py`. **Why (c) exists: (b) was not enough, measured.** Instruction-only got explained negatives from **20% to 56%**; the other 44% still told a lawyer something was not found without saying what had been looked for. Invariant 2 exactly. **ACCEPTANCE PASSED with one stated residual** (`evidence/replay/wave2_p22_final/`, n=3 on 6409 and 6367, $5.32): **21 turns asserting a negative, 19 explained (90%)**, against **2 of 10 (20%)** on the same sessions in Wave 1; terms named 20%→90%, miss attributed to the index 40%→95%, and **turns blaming the lawyer's own citation 2 → 0** — 6373's *"could you confirm the year or the SI number?"* for a correct citation, where `ssi/2026/170` and `ssi/2025/377` are both verified 404s in LEX. **The residual: both failures are turns with ZERO delegations**, a negative carried forward from an earlier turn, so no search ran and no footer fired. The footer is per-turn; a conversation is not. Left as a limitation — restating full scope on every follow-up would be noise. **State plainly that the mechanical test is now circular:** the footer satisfies the three conditions by construction, so `replay_report negatives` prints a second **model column** graded with the footer stripped. **That column is unstable and is reported as such** — it swung **56% → 4%** between two sweeps the model could not distinguish (the footer is appended after the model has finished), which n=3 cannot produce legitimately. **SIX instrument errors in this row alone, and one is a new failure mode: a product change corrupting the instrument measuring it.** The footer's own words (*"anything reported above as not found was not found in this index"*) trip `NEG_ASSERTED`, so selecting the denominator on the full answer enrolled every researched turn including purely positive ones (23 → 34). The denominator is now taken from the model's prose. The other five were all *under*-reads: a first `NEG_ASSERTED` that scored **61 of 153 turns, 100% failing** by counting *"no winding-up order may be made, except by the company's directors"* as a bare negative; a `terms` rule that demanded "searched" adjacent to "for", then demanded quotation marks; an `index` rule defeated by a 158-character instrument title (**every commencement SSI's title contains a full stop — "Commencement No. 1" — so a sentence window keyed on `.` cannot cross the titles this corpus is about**); and a `limits` condition demanding the model recite an **inert** `year_to=2026` that excluded nothing, now `n/a` where no filter could have bitten. Every correction was re-validated against Wave 1, where the before-side moved only 44/44 → 44/38 failing. **Also corrected here: P5.3's "UK SI 2026 ~0% held"** — false in the direction that matters, since `/legislation/search` returns **27 distinct `uksi/2026/*`** instruments and all 12 spot-checked resolve on lookup. A 60-point census says ~2%. **And the replacement figure was itself falsified the next day** — the same command returned **8%** for SSI 2026 on 16 Sep (1/60 vs 5/60, ordinary noise at n=60). So the product string states **bounds that survive the spread** ("roughly 85%", "under 10%"), never a point estimate and never "none". `lex_probe --coverage` now samples 60 points and **runs** the `/legislation/search` cross-check rather than recommending it, because it was still executing the census that produced the retracted headline. **Opened:** **P3.7** (`/legislation/lookup`, the deterministic held/absent test this row can only disclose around). **Decision most open to being overruled:** the footer is unconditional on any turn that searched, so it appears on positive answers too — gated on a prose detector it would fail silently, which is the trap this work keeps hitting. `AUDIT_TRACE.md` amended: `final_result` now always differs from `raw_result` for both legislation search tools, so a consumer must key on the `summarised` flag, not on the texts being unequal.

Then allow subsection-level citation (6365: "it only ever cited full sections"), and relax the one-call-per-instrument rule where the question is scoped to a single Act. **Acceptance** *(replay, n=3)*: 6396 (right enactment, provisions not found), 6365, 6348 return the provision at the granularity asked for. **Evidence:** 6396 6365 6348 6369 6333 6380 6354 6347 6372 6385. **Depends on:** P1.5, P2.* · **PRE-FLIGHT FACTS for the build session, verified at the Session 15 handover (2026-09-18, head `6256fe4`; `src/` unchanged since `bbb5416`).** **(a) The docstring claim is TRUE.** Every `search_legislation` result carries a `sections` array of `{number, provision_type, score}` — the API's own ranking of which provisions matched the query — and `_slim_search_results` (`agent/tools/lex.py:9`, claim at `:13`) drops it. Over 8,005 stored results (`wave2_p27`, `wave2_p27_pre`, `wave2_p24_final`: `audit … tools[].api_calls[].response`): 1 to 10 entries per result (median 1; 1 entry 4,047, 2 entries 1,939, 3 entries 905, 10 entries 203); `provision_type` is `section` (15,927) or `schedule` (1,495); **numbers are section-level only — no subsection anywhere** — and **1,196 entries have an empty `number`**, including at score 1.0; scores 0.105–1.0, median 0.64. **Confirmed live the same day**: `POST /legislation/search` for "Scottish Water annual report and accounts" ranks the Water Industry (Scotland) Act 2002's ss.57, 17, 45, 5, 20, 41, (empty), 42, sch 7, sch 3. So the array can tell Phase 2 WHICH sections to read; subsection-level citation must still come from the section text. **(b) `search_legislation_sections` ignores the size we send.** `executor.py:276` posts `limit: 10` to `/legislation/section/search`, whose schema parameter is **`size`** (default 10): verified live, `limit=3` returns 10 results, `size=3` returns 3, `size=20` returns 20. Harmless today only because both are 10; any change to the number of section results must use `size`. **(c) `/legislation/section/lookup`** (unused) takes `legislation_id` and `limit` and is documented as "get all sections for specific legislation" — not a lookup of one section by number. Read `/openapi.json` (`python -m tools.lex_probe --surface`) before designing around it. **(d) The one-call rule** is in `WORKER_SYSTEM_PROMPT` (`prompts.py:194`) and `WORKER_SYSTEM_PROMPT_HYBRID` (`:318`); this row's third part relaxes it, and it is also the last of D16's four unplanned items. | `[ ]` | **P3.3** | **B11 — Separate retrieval from interpretation.** **BLOCKED (user decision, 2026-10-05): parked until a lawyer answers the confirmation pack.** Its criteria rest on the pack's Readings 2 to 4 (its 6338 turn-3 criterion is held for Reading 2's supplementary questions), and its acceptance includes P3.2's, which is blocked too. Three sessions, all verified wrong or unhedged. 6338 applied the Interpretation and Legislative Reform (Scotland) Act 2010 to a 2009 Act, twice, unhedged — **ILRA s.1(1)(a) applies Part 1 only to Acts whose Bills received Royal Assent on or after Part 1 commenced** (verified at source); HeatherE was right and the correct instrument is the Scotland Act 1998 interpretation order. It was also wrong that ss.35A and 35ZA cannot run concurrently, reasoning from structure rather than text. | `[x]` | **P4.2** | **B13 — Lost turns, blank replies, latency.** **15 user turns across 11 sessions never received a reply**; two assistant messages rendered blank — 6370 #8 **billed $0.0217** with nothing shown, 6363 #6 billed nothing. 6387 timed out on a follow-up. ~~n=2 on the blanks, so reproduce before fixing.~~ ~~**Reproduced in the P0.3 baseline: 4 billed-but-empty turns (6370 ×2, 6407 ×2), all `status: ok` with `audit.error: null`.**~~ **An undercount, corrected at the build (Session 10): there are NINE blank-body turns across the ten replay directories, EIGHT of them billed — 6370 ×2, 6406, 6407 ×2, 6338, 6359, 6383, plus 6374 r3 t3 at $0. 8 billed blanks in 616 turns (1.3%), six sessions, three chat modes. `python -m tools.replay_report --dir <dir> blanks`.** **Suspect (1) is eliminated by the evidence:** none of the four emitted a single `token` event, so there was never a body for the `<suggestions>` strip to consume. Start at suspect (2). Suspect (3) is unsupported — no error event on any of the four. **The acceptance test cannot name a turn:** this row cites 6370 #8, but the replay blanked at turns 2 and 3 while the pre-pilot's turn 2 answered normally — assert the invariant (non-empty body whenever recorded cost > 0), not a session-turn pair. The *timeout* half did **not** reproduce: 6387 answered all three turns. The billed-but-empty case is the informative one: content was generated and lost downstream. Eliminate in order: (1) a `<suggestions>` strip consuming the whole body — `utils/suggestions.py` takes the **last** block and also strips an *unterminated* trailing tag, which is exactly what a partial stream looks like; (2) a tool-call-only final message; (3) the `chat_loop` stream-retry guard re-raising after partial emission. **Suspect (4), added 2026-09-15 and now the leading one: the provider returns an empty final message and we cannot see that it did.** `chat_loop` (`agent/openrouter_client.py`) accumulates `delta.content` and `delta.tool_calls` and **never reads `finish_reason`**, so an empty stream becomes `{"role": "assistant", "content": ""}` and, with no tool calls, is returned as the answer with `status: ok`, no error and full billing. That is the B13 signature exactly, and it explains "no `token` event" without requiring anything to be lost downstream. Three public reports describe this shape on this model family: Google has **reproduced and is fixing** Gemini 3 streaming + function calling ending `finish_reason=STOP` with empty text after a tool executes -- absent in unary mode and on 2.5-flash (<https://discuss.ai.google.dev/t/gemini-3-5-flash-with-streamgeneratecontent-automatic-function-calling-stops-after-tool-execution-and-returns-empty-final-text/170528>); Gemini's `MALFORMED_FUNCTION_CALL` is silently normalised to `finish_reason='stop'` with empty content and zero tool calls, *indistinguishable from the model choosing to say nothing* (<https://github.com/BerriAI/litellm/issues/21744>); and OpenRouter emits duplicate `finish_reason` chunks on tool calls, the second one empty (<https://github.com/QwenLM/qwen-code/issues/2402>). OpenRouter's own docs add that models occasionally return no content during warm-up/scaling events, and that mid-stream failures arrive as `finish_reason: 'error'` carrying `native_finish_reason` (<https://openrouter.ai/docs/api_reference/errors-and-debugging>). **This is a lead, not a diagnosis** -- those reports are the google-genai SDK with *automatic* function calling and cite 3.5-flash / 3.1-flash-lite / 3.1-pro, not our OpenRouter manual tool loop on `google/gemini-3.1-pro-preview`. **So the first move is a diagnostic, not a fix:** capture `finish_reason` and `native_finish_reason` in `chat_loop` and surface them on the audit trace (additive, fail-soft, a few lines). Until that lands all four suspects look identical in the data, which is why this row has never been able to name a cause. It also means **the fix may not be ours** -- if it is the upstream streaming bug, the answer is a bounded retry on an empty final message, not a parser change. **New evidence from the Wave 1 sweep: 6338 turn 2** -- 2 delegations, real API calls, 330 s, $0.3996, `status: ok`, no `token` event, empty body. B13 is **not confined to 6370/6407**, which is what this row was scoped against. Separately, for perceived latency (MoniqueM reported a 5.5-minute turn as "around 15 minutes"), add a coarse step counter — the `tool_call` / `api_call_start` events already exist behind `emit_tool_details` and cost nothing in retrieval terms. **Acceptance** *(deterministic)*: assert a non-empty body whenever recorded cost > 0. **Evidence:** 6387 6370 6363 6345 6335 **6338** (Wave 1 sweep). **Depends on:** — (independent of the accuracy path) · **A NEW AND WORSE INSTANCE OF THIS BUCKET, found at P2.5 (Session 9), and the code has no guard against it.** 6383 rep 1 turn 4 of P2.5's acceptance sweep returned a Deep Research report whose body was **empty** - the lawyer saw the scope footer and nothing else - with `status: ok`, no error, 30 tool calls, three intact step reports (4,836 / 5,777 / 7,190 chars) and 219 commencement relations retrieved. **First blank in 218 answered turns across eight replay directories**, and 6383's DR turn produced 9,190 / 6,503 / 7,490 / 8,672 chars on the four prior runs. **The cause is an empty provider completion at the synthesis call**, not the scope strip: the request went out at `tools=0, msgs=2, ~21421 chars` and the task finished **9 seconds later**, and `strip_scope_blocks` never fired (it logs when it does; the log has no such line). **Two gaps this exposes, both additive to fix.** (1) `chat_loop`'s stream retry cannot catch it: it retries only while nothing has been emitted, and a successful 200 carrying no content chunks raises no exception, so an empty completion is indistinguishable from a finished answer. (2) `run_deep_research` never checks that the synthesis produced anything - a blank report is composed, footered and returned. The obvious fix is a fallback to the step findings when the synthesis comes back empty, which is strictly better than nothing and needs no new prompt. **n=1 and the re-run is the test**; P2.5's prompt change to `DEEP_RESEARCH_SYNTHESIS_PROMPT` is not excluded as a contributor, but no mechanism links a longer instruction to an empty completion and an empty 200 is known provider behaviour. **Evidence:** 6383. · **DONE (Session 10). The cause is an empty provider completion, and the code could not see one.** The diagnostic landed first as the row required: `finish_reason`, `native_finish_reason`, `reasoning_chars`, `completion_tokens` and a mid-stream `error` payload are now captured in both clients and surfaced as **audit schema v3's `empty_completions[]`** (`[]` on a healthy request; `AUDIT_TRACE.md` updated). They separate the four mechanisms the stored evidence could not tell apart — and the `error` payload is the one that was **ours**: OpenRouter reports a mid-stream failure that way and `chat_loop` read only `usage` and `choices`, so such a stream ended as an ordinary empty completion with `status: ok`. **The question the row posed about 6383 is answered and it changed the fix:** 6383's synthesis call declared NO tools (`tools=0, msgs=2`), so the failure is **not** confined to tool-call turns and a guard scoped to them would have missed it — the retry keys on an empty completion, full stop. It is stochastic, not deterministic (same payload succeeded five times, failed once), which is the condition a bounded retry answers. **Built:** (a) `chat_loop` in BOTH clients retries a clean 200 carrying no content and no tool calls, reusing `_MAX_STREAM_ATTEMPTS`/backoff; a tool-call-only message is never retried (that is the normal ReAct shape and replaying it re-runs the turn's research), and the discarded attempt's cost is banked so the turn's recorded spend stays honest. (b) `run_deep_research` falls back to the step findings, (c) the Manager falls back to the worker reports it already holds, or to a plain notice when there are none — **both labelled as fallbacks under Invariant 1**, since research the answering step never used is not an answer and must not read like one. **The latency half is built too:** a step count and an elapsed clock in the status line, both derived client-side from data already arriving (`tool_start` events, the run's existing `startedAt`) — no new SSE events, no backend change. **No denominator, deliberately**: outside Deep Research nothing knows the total, so "step 3 of 8" would be a claim about how much is left. **Acceptance PASSED:** `replay_report blanks` asserts the invariant over stored run files (exit 1 on a violation) and reproduces the hand count exactly on all ten directories. It grades the **body**, not the answer — since P2.2 a blank turn is 1,293 characters of scope footer with nothing above it, and grading `answer` scores that turn as fine. Validated both directions: the shortest bodies it leaves alone are real 46–50 char answers. **998 tests** (963 → 998). Live-verified end to end: a real research turn returned a full report, and the status line read *"Researching · 13 steps · 1m 01s"*. **B13 closes.** **Residual found at P2.8 (Session 13), now row P4.5:** the retry and both fallbacks exist at the Manager and Deep Research seams, but not at the WORKER's. A worker whose final completion is lost hands the Manager a report with no body.

| `[x]` | **P4.4** | **Remove the case-law court filter.** *(New row, Session 5, from P5.2's probe — and DONE in the same session.)* **Three reasons, and the third made it a defect rather than dead weight.** **(1) Nobody used it** — zero of the 41 replayed pre-pilot sessions set a court, against `current_only` at 29 and `jurisdiction` at 13; only 11 of 62 sessions touched case law at all. **(2) For a Scottish Government audience it was a trap** — all 14 options are English, Welsh or UK-wide, because the National Archives corpus holds no Scottish courts at all (`court=csoh` → **400, "not one of the available choices"**, P5.2), so selecting any of them guaranteed a non-Scottish result set and narrowed a corpus that was already the wrong jurisdiction. **(3) It OVERRODE the model** — `executor.py` applied `_court` *after* the model's own `court` argument and clobbered it, so a court chosen three turns earlier silently beat the model's per-query judgement: the same stale-filter failure as B2 and B4. Removed: the UI control and its `COURT_GROUPS` vocabulary, `caseLawCourt` state, its localStorage keys and per-chat snapshot field, the filter chip, the request field on all three routers' shared model, `_court`, the executor override, the prompt's constraint line and `_COURT_LABELS`. **Kept deliberately:** the tool schema's court codes; `redact.py`'s `court` (a model tool arg, still sent); the **export column** and historical `filters` rows, because pre-pilot snapshots recorded real values and an export that dropped them would misrepresent what those sessions ran under; and `court` in the replay harness, which records historical configuration. **The jurisdiction panel's Scotland/NI note stays** — it is the true statement the court list was quietly contradicting. **Acceptance passed** *(deterministic)*: `tests/test_court_filter_removed.py` (9 tests — field gone, stale client ignored not rejected, neighbours intact, no prompt line, no stale-key resurrection, override gone, **tool parameter and model-supplied pass-through both still present**, audit null), 643 tests green, `client/dist` rebuilt. **Note what this does NOT fix:** removing a misleading control does not put the Court of Session in the corpus. **P2.4 is still required** and P5.2's question still stands. **Depends on:** — |

Over 50 directories and 930 schema-v3 turns, the 34 empty calls split into four mechanisms. **(a) reasoned to nothing:** 8 calls (15 attempts), 2 recovered, every one in a research Worker and every one in the conversational quick-lookup Worker: 8 of 478 conversational worker runs against 0 of 522 in the other chat modes (one-sided Fisher 0.0026; 4 of the 8 are session 6348, so they come from 5 sessions). The 8 turns cost $12.38 and 7,073 s over their slots' clean medians (median per turn $1.33 and 902 s), against $156.55 for all v3 turns. **(b) upstream idle timeout:** 11 calls (32 attempts), 3 recovered, $0.29 and 3,143 s over the medians (median 371 s): a latency cost, not a spend one. **(c) clean stop, no reasoning:** 2 calls, both Deep Research steps, no excess. **(d) upstream rate limit** (new): 13 calls, all recovered, no excess. The retry after an (a) attempt answered 2 times in 12 (after (b) 3 in 22, after (d) 13 in 14), and the Manager's re-delegation made good 6 of 6 unrecovered (a) worker calls. **Probes.** The model's output ceiling is 65,536 tokens and its reasoning is mandatory. With no `reasoning` field it reasons in the `high` range, about twice `medium`. `reasoning.max_tokens` lands on an effort level, not a budget. A top-level `max_tokens` binds. On the seam, both `wave3_p313`/6348 payloads (r2 t1, r3 t3) rebuild to exactly their recorded `sent_chars`, and both re-drew a runaway to 62,916 reasoning tokens: r2 t1 came back empty (370 s, $0.77), r3 t3 answered after 310 s ($0.77). **With `max_tokens` 16,000 the r3 t3 draw came back empty at 15,360 tokens (96% of the cap, as 62,912 is of 65,536) in 93 s for $0.20. A cap ends the failure sooner; it does not make the model answer.** Lowering the effort did not bound it: on r2 t1, 3 of 4 draws at `medium` or `low` came back empty (two (a), one (b)), and the one answer missed s.36(2). **Spend on the pre-flight: $4.35** ($0.93 on the tiny probe calls, including the committed re-run; $3.42 on seam draws). **ACCEPTANCE BOOKED (Session 28, 2026-09-25, before any product code).** **Build.** (1) `chat_loop` in both clients takes `worker_call` (default False) and forwards it through its ReAct recursion and P3.8's write-up round. `run_worker_agent` passes True on its research loop only, so the Manager, the planner, the Deep Research synthesis and the A4 reformat are unchanged; a Deep Research step is a worker run and is covered. (2) Lever (ii), both clients: on a Worker call, an empty completion that reasoned heavily (`is_heavy_empty`: 10,000 or more completion tokens, or 20,000 or more reasoning characters, the instrument's own (a) threshold) is not retried. It returns empty at once into P4.5's lost-report label, and its record says `retried: false`. A light empty ((b), (c), (d)) is retried as before, and a non-worker call retries every empty as before. (3) The cap, OpenRouter only: a Worker call's payload carries `max_tokens: 32000` (`WORKER_MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS`), and every other payload is unchanged. Ollama accepts the flag and applies (ii) only, because `num_predict` on its cloud models is unmeasured. (4) A capped call that returns content with `finish_reason=length` is logged as possibly cut (a watch item; the bound below says it should be rare). | `[x]` | **P4.19** | **Retry case-law calls on a rate limit, as LEX calls are.** *(New row, 2026-10-02, from a review of the National Archives API against its published spec (`nationalarchives.github.io/ds-find-caselaw-docs/public`); booked at the user's decision, outside a numbered session.)* The National Archives publishes a limit of **1,000 requests per rolling five minutes per IP address**, answered with HTTP 429. The target is one IP for every user, every Worker and every Deep Research step. `search_case_law` (`executor.py`) and `_fetch_judgment_text` (`caselaw.py`, behind `get_case_law_text`) call `client.get` directly, not `_request_with_retry` (A5a), so a 429 reaches `raise_for_status()` and the retrieval is dropped: the contained worker error reports a failed step, or the answer is silently thinner. No 429 was observed in the pre-pilot (a handful of users); the row is preventive, for pilot scale. **The fix:** route both calls through `_request_with_retry`, which already honours `Retry-After` (capped at 30 s) and backs off on 429/502/503/504. **Note (batch 5 C):** the helper also retries a timeout or transport error up to three times, which neither call does today, so say in the tests whether that is wanted at the case-law calls' 15 s timeout; and `_fetch_judgment_text` opens its own client, so it needs one passed in or built for the helper. **Acceptance** *(deterministic)*: unit tests with a mocked 429-then-200 for each of the two calls, each failing with the change reverted; a 400 (an invalid court code) still returns the existing error immediately, unretried. **Evidence:** the published limit (`public_api.yml`). **Depends on:** — **Build and licence (user decision, 2026-10-02, batch 5 C2, C3):** one build with P3.9, P3.22, P3.23 and P4.19, in the order P3.22's row gives; the National Archives licence (`docs/TODO.md` D23) is booked and does not block it. **Built and accepted (Session 39, 2026-10-05, batch 7 D, `notes/batch7_D.md`).** `search_case_law` and `_fetch_judgment_text` (which now takes the executor's client) go through `_request_with_retry`, 15 s per request kept; a 400 or 404 returns at once, unretried. Timeouts and transport errors are retried too (user decision, kept as built: the only failures in 1,713 stored calls were 3 DNS errors, none near 15 s). Acceptance met: 8 tests, 6 fail with the change reverted (40 lines; 4 for the search and 2 for the fetch, each half alone), re-run by the integrator. |

(plural: "were looked up by their numbers and are not held ... whether the numbers are accurate"), chosen over a shorter form without "which is incomplete" and one adding "recent instruments least of all"; "accurate", not "right", which `OPENER_VOCAB` reads.
